# Semantic Blocks Plan — Pre-flight Conflict Scan

THIS IS A DRAFT DOCUMENT

Scan of the implementation plan in this directory (README.md, phase-00 … phase-10) against the spec (`docs/drafts/semantic-blocks.md`) and the real source tree of this worktree, before any task runs. Nothing in the plan, the spec or the tree was changed; this file is the only output.

**How it was checked.** Every task's plan text was read in full and verified against the files it names. The checks were:

- tests against the implementation they test (names, signatures, includes, expected values recomputed where cheap);
- CMake registration and the staged-file set;
- the test binary each run line targets, and whether its "Expected: FAIL" is plausible;
- the Global Constraints (README:35-58);
- whether each task's edit anchors still apply after every earlier task touching the same file — 319 (task, file) checks, each a hunk-level comparison against the earlier tasks' plan text;
- every consumed cross-task interface against what the producing task's plan text actually implements, not just the README contract.

`file:L` references below are plan line numbers (`phase-NN-….md:L`) or source lines in this worktree at `e8bab8ce` (tree `632cf37c` + plan commits).

**Counts.**

- Task rows: 133. The coordinator's "132" counts one fewer: the phase files carry 133 task headings, namely 0.1–0.2, 1.1–1.14, 2.1–2.11, 3.1–3.12, 4.1–4.14 plus 4.13a, 5.1–5.20, 6.1–6.10, 7.1–7.13, 8.1–8.9 plus 8.6a, 9.1–9.13 and 10.1–10.13.
- Shared-surface rows: 1219.
  - 883 task pairs share at least one file; 6 of them conflict.
  - 336 producer → consumer interface rows; 22 of them are not OK.
- Hunk checks: 319. Of these, 226 are OK, 87 DRIFT (only line numbers moved) and 6 CONFLICT.
- Findings: 85 — 4 Blocker, 13 Important, 68 Minor.

**Blockers (a task cannot succeed as written):** F1 — 1.7; F2 — 4.8; F3 — 9.7 (with 6.7, 6.8, 6.9); F4 — 7.11, 10.5.

## 2. Shared surfaces

### 2.2 Task pairs that touch the same file

One row per pair of tasks that touch at least one common file (A earlier than B). The columns are:

- **A produces**: whether A creates or edits each shared file.
- **B consumes/expects**: for each shared file, whether B's hunk anchors on text A wrote (or overlaps A's region) or is independent of it.
- **Finding**: the result of checking B's anchors against the file as every earlier task leaves it.

"Line refs drift" means a cited `file:L` moved but the quoted text anchor is intact; see F18. No file is created twice.

## 3. Specific checks

### (a) Does the default right-hand scrollbar cover the sticky header's chip or the last grid column?

**Verdict: yes, both.** The chip overlap is new and needs a fix (F11). The last-column overlap is the
existing overlay design and needs none.

**Measurements from the tree:**

- **The scrollbar is an overlay.** `SessionChrome.qml:117-120` says "Overlay only — no gutter is reserved, so the
  grid/window geometry is untouched".
  - Strip: `implicitWidth: 12`, `padding: 2` logical px, placed at `x: parent.width - vbar.width` (:102).
  - Handle: `implicitWidth: 8` (:145), at opacity 0.28 at rest, 0.45 on hover (:150). The thumb band is
    therefore x ∈ [W−10, W−2].
  - The bar is shown only while there is history (`hasScrollback`, :104, :142). It takes presses across its whole
    strip.
- **Nothing reserves its width.** `pageSizeForPixels` (`src/contour/geometry/WindowGeometry.hpp:226-238`)
  subtracts margins and the gutter only. No file in geometry/, display/ or window/ reads `ScrollBarConfig`.
- **A cell is narrower than the strip.** At the default 12 pt (`src/vtrasterizer/FontDescriptions.hpp:48`) a cell
  is the font's advance (`src/vtrasterizer/Renderer.cpp:45-51`), about 9.6 logical px at 96 dpi. The strip is
  therefore about 1.25 cells and the thumb about 0.83 cell.
- **Today the bar is hidden by default** (`Config.hpp:411`); Task 8.6a makes it `Right`. `Right` already overlays the
  last column rather than narrowing the grid.
- **The chip ends flush right.** Phase 7 places it at `chipStart = columns − renderTextWidth(chip)`
  (phase-07:3031-3036; test :2929-2936). The header text stops at `columns − chipWidth − 1` (:2302-2303), where the
  `−1` is the blank gap column, not an inset. The chip's last glyph is therefore in the last column, under the
  thumb whenever the view is near the top of history. That is always the case for spec §6.3's top-of-scrollback
  placeholder. Task 8.6a only "accepts" this (phase-08:2244-2251).
- **Smallest fix:** a two-column chip inset, `StickyHeaderChipInset`. It clears the 12-px strip for any cell at
  least 6 px wide. The full ruling is in F11.
- **Last column: no change.** Reserving the strip in geometry would take a column from every shell. The overlay is
  the documented design the owner chose (spec §6.1; 8.6a:2244-2249).

### (d) Engine-default ruling: vtbackend gutter switches off, GutterConfig on

**Verdict: fails in one task (4.8, F2); clean everywhere else.**

**Task 4.8.** Four new `Gutter.terminal.*` tests set only `GutterSettings { .foldMarkers = … }` but expect
exit-status glyphs and colours or the ◆ user mark. With engine defaults off they cannot pass:
`aBlockIsColouredByHowItEnded` (phase-04:2915), `aRunningCommandShowsATriangle` (:2938),
`withFoldMarkersOffAFinishedHeadShowsTheDot` (:2955) and `aUserMarkShowsADiamondOverTheFoldBody` (:2967).

**Everywhere else:**

- 4.1–4.7: every test sets the switches it needs. Folding_test's six `settings().foldMarkers` uses are migrated
  explicitly in 4.6.
- 4.9–4.13: the remaining `Gutter.terminal` tests set what they need.
- Session and config tests reach the switches through `emulationSettings` / `configureTerminal` (wired in 4.12), so
  they get the `GutterConfig` defaults (on), as intended.
- No test in phases 5, 7, 8, 9 or 10 relies on a vtbackend gutter switch.

**Stale wording** that still describes the superseded "defaults on" draft: F43, F45, F46.

### (e) "Evicted placeholder anywhere" (phase 7) and "scrollbar default right" (8.6a, phase 10) vs spec §6.1/§6.3 and each other

**Verdict: consistent.** The four gaps are wording only.

**Evicted placeholder.** Spec §6.3 (spec:393-406) says the placeholder shows:

- at the very top of the scrollback always, independent of `mode`;
- elsewhere wherever the mode shows a header;
- nowhere when `show_evicted: false`.

Phase 7 implements exactly this:

- Decision 2 (phase-07:23-36);
- the pure rule `showEvicted && (topRowAge == Oldest || modeShowsHeader(mode, viewport))` (:578-585);
- the fill's early-out `mode == Never && !showEvicted` (:2285);
- tests at :296-377 and Task 7.7 (:2407-2502).

README C3/C9 (`showEvicted = true`) agree, as do phase 10's settings row (:1156-1162), feature page (:2002-2005) and
release note (:2342).

**Scrollbar default.** Spec §6.1 (spec:369-372) and §11.1 (spec:593) set `position` to `right`.

- Task 8.6a sets `ScrollBarConfig::position` to `Right` (the tree has `Hidden` at `Config.hpp:411`). Its writer
  spelling `"Right"` matches `std::formatter<ScrollBarPosition>` (`Config.hpp:2539`).
- Phase 10 asserts and documents the same default: Task 10.7 (:1539-1541, :1645-1648), the feature page
  (:1973-1984) and Task 10.11 (:2321-2323, :2331).

**How the two meet.** Every header kind, the placeholder included, carries the right-aligned chip, which the default
bar overlaps. That is check (a).

**Wording gaps:**

- F65: spec:45 is narrower than the amended §6.3.
- F71: README:499 says the scrollbar is "unchanged", and phase-10:1868 "behaves as it always has". Both are
  no longer literally true.
- F72: the README Phase 8 amendment does not record the `Right` default.
- Spec §6.2 and phase 7 Decision 8 do not mention the scrollbar's press exception. That ruling is folded into
  F11.

### (f) Phase 2's adopt/notification behaviour vs phase 6's "no notification on attach snapshot"

**Verdict: they agree on all four questions.**

| Question | Phases 1/2 | Phase 6 / spec | Agree |
|---|---|---|---|
| Which mode returns a summary | `adopt(…, Live)` returns one only for a `Finished` record with `end == Reported` whose id the store did not already hold as `Finished`. `Snapshot` never returns one (phase-01:1476-1503; tests :1321-1355). `Terminal::adoptCommandBlock` raises `commandBlockFinished` exactly when `adopt` returns a summary (phase-02:584-588). | Phase 6 posts every raised summary and filters nothing itself (phase-06:7, 2707-2713). Spec §12 says Snapshot raises no events (spec:641-642). | ✓ |
| `Live` adopt of an already-finished record | An absent id announces once. An identical resend returns early, and a changed copy of a held `Finished` record sets `wasFinished` (phase-01:1482-1484). | A command that finished during a resize resync still notifies (spec:642-643; tested phase-02:2627-2636). | ✓ |
| Re-adopt of the same id (reconnect, resend) | No duplicate. A reattach is a fresh `NativeController` and `ScreenMirror` (`src/contour/remote/NativeController.hpp:350`, `NativeController.cpp:429`), so its first replay is `Prime`, which adopts as `Snapshot` (phase-02:2692-2695, 2781-2783). | Spec:648-650: "reattaching never replays notifications". | ✓ |
| A record that finished while detached | Adopted as `Snapshot`, so silent (phase-02:22, 2638-2653). | Phase 6 needs nothing of its own. | ✓ |

**Findings:**

- F28: README:446-447 is narrower than phase 2's D4. D4 also adopts as `Snapshot` a never-held record older
  than the newest one held, and the survivors of a retirement. A strict executor could break phase 2's test at
  phase-02:2603.
- F35: the "reattaching never replays" test covers a reattach only because every attach builds a fresh mirror,
  and the plan does not say so.
- Related, in phase 2's head tracking rather than notifications: F6. It compares `Grid::generation()` where
  every consumer uses `stableIdGeneration()`.

### (g) Every new file has an SPDX line and is clang-formatted before its commit

**Verdict: pass.** The plan creates 71 files: 45 `.cpp`, 24 `.hpp`, 1 `.pwsh` and 1 `.md`.

**C++ files (all 69).**

- Each file's code block opens with `// SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0`.
- Each file is named in a `clang-format -i` step before its commit's staging line. Three tasks name their files
  generically: 5.13 ("every file touched in this task", phase-05:4313), 5.19 ("the three new files", :5832) and 9.6
  (phase-09:1920).
- Each new `.hpp` carries `#pragma once`.

**The other two files.**

- `shell-integration.pwsh` (3.9, phase-03:2406) carries `#// SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0`. That is exactly
  the form of the existing `shell-integration.zsh/.fish/.tcsh:3`; clang-format does not apply to it.
- `docs/demo/command-blocks.md` (10.9) carries no SPDX line, which matches every existing `docs/**/*.md`; none has
  one.

**One related gap:** a modified (not new) file is missing from a format step. Task 5.14 edits and stages
`ConfigDocumentation.hpp` but leaves it out of its `clang-format -i` list (F52).


## 4. Findings

Severity: **Blocker** = a task cannot succeed as written; **Important** = rework or a review failure; **Minor** = text fix or drift an executor would handle. Each ruling is the smallest concrete change to the plan text. Where a finding was reported by more than one check it appears once, and the other reports are named in it.

**Coordinator rulings (2026-10-09):** every proposed ruling accepted and applied to the plan; alternatives chosen are noted in the Ruling column.
